Step-level detail for W-M1.1 to W-M1.6 follows the same shape (test first, run through the slot and watch it fail, implement, run, commit on the branch); the first three are written out here because they fix interfaces the rest consume.

**W-M1.1 pgraph symbols sidecar.** Interfaces produced: `type Symbol struct{ ID, Pkg, File string; Line int; Doc string }`; `func Symbols(p *Program) []Symbol` (sorted by ID; File repo-relative; Doc the first sentence of the doc comment, ASCII-folded, at most 160 bytes, empty when none); `func EncodeSymbols(syms []Symbol, digestsSum string) []byte` (header line `pgraph-symbols v0`, second line `digests\t<sha256 hex of pgraph.digests bytes>`, then one tab-separated row per symbol); written by `SyncRoot` next to `pgraph.digests`; the walk file's line set grows by one entry for the symbols file so the vouch chain covers it.
- [ ] Step 1: in `pgraph/symbols_test.go` write `TestSymbols_PlacesEveryDeclared` (load `pgraph/testdata`, assert every `declares` object in `Extract` has a Symbol with a non-empty File and Line > 0), `TestSymbols_DocFirstSentence` (a fixture function with a two-sentence doc yields the first sentence only), `TestSymbols_EncodeChainsToDigests` (second line equals `digests\t` plus the sha256 of the digests bytes passed in).
- [ ] Step 2: run through the slot: `docs-out/plans/slot/goslot2.sh 'cd pgraph && GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.27.3 go test ./ -run TestSymbols_ -count=1'`; expect FAIL with undefined `Symbols`.
- [ ] Step 3: implement `Symbols` over `p.pkgs` using `go/packages` syntax and `TypesInfo.Defs` with the existing namer from `identity.go`; `Doc` from the `ast.FuncDecl.Doc` or `GenDecl.Doc` first sentence (`go/doc.Synopsis` replacement verified at this step, R14); implement `EncodeSymbols`; call both from `SyncRoot` after digests are written; add the symbols path to the walk file writer.
- [ ] Step 4: rerun Step 2; expect PASS; run `go vet ./...` in `pgraph`.
- [ ] Step 5: `git add pgraph/symbols.go pgraph/symbols_test.go pgraph/syncroot.go && git commit -m "pgraph: symbols sidecar chained to digests (W-M1.1)"`.

**W-M1.2 `platform.ReadSnapshot`.** Interface: `type Snapshot struct{ Facts []Fact; Places []Place; Files map[string][]string }` (intentional change, principal's call pending the owner's word, 2026-11-05 00:0x: `platform.Symbol` already names the by-name read's row type, so the place row is `Place`; a Snapshot is returned only when vouched, so there is no Vouched field; `ErrNotSynced` names a root with no walk file or one from a pgraph that wrote no symbols file, `ErrUnvouched` a file the walk does not vouch for) where `Fact` is the exported form of the existing `fact{subject, predicate, object, tier, config}` and `Files` maps a package path to its repo-relative files from the digests sidecar; `func ReadSnapshot(ctx context.Context, root string) (Snapshot, error)` reads walk, digests, log and symbols; refuses with `ErrUnvouched` when the walk digest or the symbols' chained digest does not match; bounded by `MaxLogBytes`.
- [ ] Step 1: `internal/platform/snapshot_test.go`: `TestReadSnapshot_Vouched` (a sidecar set written by the real pgraph on `pgraph/testdata` reads with Vouched true and the symbol count equal to the declares count), `TestReadSnapshot_RefusesStaleSymbols` (flip one byte in the symbols file; expect `ErrUnvouched` and an empty Snapshot).
- [ ] Step 2: run through the slot; expect FAIL on undefined `ReadSnapshot`.
- [ ] Step 3: implement by composing `readVouch`, `readDigests`, `readLog` (nil hook and keep) and a new `readSymbols`; export `Fact`.
- [ ] Step 4: PASS; add the disclosure paragraph in `internal/disclose/paragraphs.go` naming the symbols file as a read.
- [ ] Step 5: commit.

**W-M1.3 `lang/gopg` resolver.** Interface: `type Resolver struct{ Root string }`; `Lang() = "go"`; `Claims(rel) = strings.HasSuffix(rel, ".go")`; `Unit(rel) = "unit:go:" + path.Dir(rel)`; `Extract(root, files, rev)`: calls `platform.ReadSnapshot`, errors with "run pgraph sync" when the sidecars are absent, then maps: each file to a `file:` node with `digest` and `lang` attrs and a `Contains` edge from its unit; each Symbol to `sym:go:<unit>:<symkind>:<qualified>` with attrs `name`, `symkind`, `line`, `doc`, and a `Declares` edge from the file; `calls` to `Calls` (PROVEN, Certain true), `may-call` to `MayCall` (ALGORITHMIC, Certain false), `implements` and `implements-via-pointer` to `ResolvesTo`, `embeds` to `References`; the package-to-directory map from `Snapshot.Files` resolves pgraph's `PKG.Name` ids to units; symbols outside the index root are kept without a file (unplaced). `Overlay` returns nil, nil in M1, the interface's own signal for a language admitted without overlays (intentional change, principal's call, 2026-11-05 00:1x: lang.Resolver's doc rules, and an error would record a gap at every Go edit); W-M2.5 adds it. As built, `platform.Snapshot` also carries `Digests map[file]sha256` so a file node's digest is the content pgraph read, not the file now; an id the facts name that no place row holds (an interface's methods) becomes an unplaced sym node declared by its unit; an id outside the synced packages (the standard library, another module) is dropped with its edge.
- [ ] Step 1: `gopg_test.go` over `pgraph/testdata` sidecars: `TestGopg_UnitIsDirectory`, `TestGopg_RecordsForFixture` (counts of file, sym nodes and Calls, MayCall edges equal the sidecar's), `TestGopg_TiersPreserved` (no PROVEN edge from a may-call row), `TestGopg_ErrorsWithoutSync`.
- [ ] Step 2: FAIL; Step 3: implement; register in `lang.Registry` from `index`; Step 4: PASS, `TestEngine_NoLanguageLiterals` still passes (the engine is untouched); Step 5: commit.

**W-M1.4 to W-M1.6** follow the same five steps; their tests by name: `TestBuild_TwoLanguages`, `TestBuild_UnitLangRequired`; `TestTokens_CamelCase` (`PlatformRead` yields `platform`, `read`), `TestScope_DocMatches` (a prompt word in a symbol's doc scores the symbol); `TestImport_LedgerRow` (a nine-field row yields id `B227`, kind lesson, clause within 160 bytes, source file and row), `TestImport_DecisionLine` (`- D161 ...` yields kind decision), `TestImport_ClauseValidates` (every clause passes `slot.Approved.Validate`, else title-only and reported), `TestImport_AppliesToResolved` (paths the row names that exist in the index become applies_to; others are dropped and counted), `TestImport_Idempotent` (a second import changes nothing).

**W-M1.4 as built (2026-11-05 00:3x, principal's call pending the owner's word).** `index.Unit.Lang` is required and names the resolver (`gopg.Lang`, `pylite.Lang`); `index.Build(root, commit, c)` returns `index.Result{Store, Header, Files}` and runs each declared language's resolver over the files under that language's units only (a file outside every unit of its language, and every file of an undeclared language, is not indexed; a Go config unit is a module root to read the sync for, and the graph's Go units are the sync's package directories, so rashomon's `.rashomon/graph.json` lists `.` and `pgraph` and `unit:go:.` stays a declared node with no package behind it); the `repo:<rid>` node carries attrs `rid` and `ephemeral`. `graph.Header{Schema, Repo, Rev{Commit}, Producers, Records}` is the file's first line under those json names; `graph.Save/SaveFile` take the header and refuse one with no repo or commit; `graph.Load` refuses another schema; `graph.GlobalID`, `graph.SplitGlobal`, `graph.RepoNodeID`. `baseline.RepoID(root) (rid, ephemeral)` reads the url of the remote named origin from .git/config (through a worktree's gitdir pointer and commondir) with no git process (H-17) and normalizes it (`baseline.NormalizeOrigin`, each Part B2 form tested); intentional change: the `local:<root-commit>` form is not produced, because the root commit sits behind packed objects this program does not read and a form that changed when git packed them would be no identity, so every repository with no origin gets the ephemeral `path:` form and the aliases re-key of Part B2 covers it. The disclosure paragraph for the graph index's reads (the configuration, the units' files, .pgraph/, .git/config's origin url) and the prompt-time read of .rashomon/graph.jsonl was written here rather than in W-M1.9, as the per-task tripwire asks; ScopeVersion stays 5 under its unreleased-release rule. Open for W-M1.9: the brief's evidence file under the store's graph directory holds the prompt tokens Scope matched, which the paragraph now says and D160's prompt-never-stored rule should rule on. Tests: `TestBuild_TwoLanguages`, `TestBuild_UnitLangRequired`, `TestBuild_UnitScopesItsLanguage`, `TestBuild_GoNeedsASync`, `TestIdentity_TwoRepos`, `TestGlobalID_RoundTrip`, `TestGlobalID_RejectsMalformed`, `TestHeader_RoundTrip`, `TestLoad_RefusesOtherSchema`, `TestNormalizeOrigin_Forms`, `TestRepoID_ReadsTheOriginThroughAWorktree`, `TestRepoID_PathFormWithoutAnOrigin`, `TestAI3_ScreenSaysGraphIndexReadsTheOrigin`.

**W-M1.5 as built (2026-11-05 00:4x).** Verified at task open: `engine.Tokens` already split a name where a lower-case letter meets a capital, so the plan's `PlatformRead` example passed before any change and R3's premise (snake_case only) was wrong in that part; the measured gap was the initialism boundary (`HTTPServer` read as one word `httpserver`, `ReadJSONFile` as `read`, `jsonfil`, `HMACKey` as `hmacke`) and the unread `doc` attribute. As built: the last capital of a run of two or more starts the next word, except a lower-case s that ends the run (`URLs`, `IDs`), which stays its plural (`pluralS`); `FieldsOf` reads `doc` with `sig`, `value`, `text` and `cols` into Summary, so a doc sentence meets the prompt at the summary's weight, the rule Part B1 sets for `summary` too. The GS-6 mutation anchor on Scope's knowledge guard is untouched. Known limits, unchanged: a digit inside a name keeps the name one token (`SHA256Sum`); the stemmer's short-word rule keeps `ids` apart from `id`. Tests: `TestTokens_CamelCase` (eleven forms including the prompt side), `TestScope_DocMatches` (a doc-only hit scores the symbol as a row below SeedFloor, with the hits named, and a symbol with no doc scores nothing; FieldsOf keeps the doc out of Name).

**W-M1.6 as built (2026-11-05 01:1x, principal's call pending the owner's word).** `internal/graph/knowledge` holds row schema v1 of Part B6 with every lifecycle field present and empty from the first import (`knowledge.Row`, `File{schema, rows}`, `NodeID(kind, id)` = the kind's prefix les:, dec:, inc:, conv:, exc:, flow:, obj: on the source's own id), `ReadDir`, `WriteFile`, `Records(rows, rid, rev)`, three parsers and `Import(root, sources, store, rid, rev) (Report, error)`: intentional change from the plan's `Import(index, files)`, because the verb resolves against the saved graph (`graph.LoadFile`) and its header's rid and commit rather than an index it rebuilds, then indexes again so graph.jsonl carries the rows. Formats read: a failure ledger's nine-field rows (what happened the title, the gap field the clause, the row the text) and, found at the first run over the real ledger, its prose rows `id when who: text` (80 of the ledger's 216 id lines; the first sentence the title and clause); a notes file's `- D161 (...)` lines (wrapped lines joined, the first sentence the clause), `## D66 (...): title` headings (the paragraph the text) and headings that end at the parenthesis (`## D64 amended (...)`, the paragraph's first sentence the title); a Nygard ADR directory (`NNNN-title.md`; Accepted approved and DECLARED, Deprecated retired, Superseded superseded, else candidate and INFERRED; the Decision section the text) with no corpus in this repository. Detection is by content: a directory is records, a file with at least one ledger row is a ledger, any other file holds decision lines (the first detector read a notes file holding bare `B48 ...` prose lines as an empty ledger; corrected before the commit). Clauses: the approved-text alphabet kept, double quotes, backticks and non-ASCII dropped, a quote that is no apostrophe dropped, cut at a sentence end past 40 bytes or the last space within 160, then `slot.Approved.Validate`; a refusal keeps the title and the report names the reason. applies_to: path-like words of the title and text (a letter and a slash or a short extension; `./` and a `:line` suffix dropped) resolve against the index's file nodes (relative path, or a base name only one file carries) and unit nodes; symbols are not resolved in M1 (R4: paths only); unresolved path-like words are counted per row. Records: a node per row of a known kind not retired or superseded, INFERRED or DECLARED from the row, attrs title, text, clause, source (the file's base name), status, tier; applies_to edges only for global ids under this repository's rid (another repository's wait for the workspace, M6). `engine.IsKnowledge` covers the seven kinds so a flow or objective never seeds a traversal; `index.Build` reads the directory after the configuration's declared knowledge (`declared`). The verb lives in `cmd/rashomon/graph.go` (no `graph_knowledge.go`; the file stays under LAW10) with the graph verb's lines on the usage screen; `.rashomon/knowledge/` is ignored in rashomon's own repository so imported docs-out text stays out of git. Disclosure: the graph index paragraph names the knowledge directory read and the verb's reads and writes (TestAI3_). Measured on rashomon's own corpus at 8ff5f0aa (the worktree synced and indexed: 1,375 files, 7,128 nodes, 14,415 edges in 95 ms; 7,493 nodes and 14,518 edges after the import): FAILURE-LEDGER.txt 213 lessons, 201 with a clause (94 percent), 3 unparsed (wrapped rows); APPROACH-NOTES.md 152 decisions, 144 with a clause (95 percent); 20 title-only rows across both (9 the banned word rashomon, 7 secret-shaped words such as commit ids, 1 each system, instruction, ignore, a URL scheme); applies_to 39 ids on 31 lessons and 64 on 41 decisions, 440 and 191 path-like words not in the index; a second import byte-identical. AC-M1.3's three conditions hold on this corpus (every applies_to is an index node by construction). Drift (repaired here): the disclosure paragraph's `rashomon graph index`, written in W-M1.4, failed TRU2c (every command a paragraph names must be on the usage screen) from c9cef2b2 to 8ff5f0aa, because the W-M1.4 and W-M1.5 gates ran the cmd package's named tests only; the usage lines close it and the standing gate runs the whole cmd package. Tests: `TestImport_LedgerRow`, `TestImport_DecisionLine`, `TestImport_ADRDirectory`, `TestImport_ClauseValidates`, `TestImport_AppliesToResolved`, `TestImport_Idempotent`, `TestBuild_ReadsKnowledgeDir`, `TestIsKnowledge_EveryJudgmentKind`, `TestGraphKnowledgeImport_RowsReachTheIndex`, `TestAI3_`, `TestTRU2c_`, `TestArguments_EveryListedCommandRefusesHelp`.

**W-M1.7 as built (2026-11-05 01:4x, principal's call pending the owner's word).** The contract types of Part B6, type and test only, no behaviour on any hook path. `internal/events` (new): `events.Event{Kind, Form, Seq, Session, Agent{Name, Version}, At, Repo, Payload json.RawMessage}` under the json names kind, form, seq, session, agent, at, repo, payload; the ten kinds (session_start, prompt, plan, act_declared, act_landed, act_failed, stop, stop_failed, session_end, boundary) and the five boundary forms (compact, resume, clear, subagent_start, subagent_stop) as closed lists (`Kinds`, `Boundaries`, `Known`); intentional change: the qualifier of a session start (its source) and of a boundary (its form) is one field, `Form`, rather than two sub-structures, because an event has at most one. The adapter side is `hookio.EventKind(hookEvent, source) (kind, form, ok)`, the one place Claude Code's event names meet the vocabulary: a SessionStart whose source is compact, resume or clear is a boundary of that form (the observation of a compaction R6 relies on); the subagent events map to boundaries ahead of their subscription (W-M4.3); plan is produced by nothing until W-M2.6. `events` imports nothing of the tree and `hookio` imports `events`, never the reverse. `graph.Predicates()` (new file `internal/graph/predicates.go`) is the closed vocabulary of 25: the 16 in use plus publishes, subscribes, schedules, migrates, same_as, supersedes, contradicts, advances, step; `graph.IsPredicate`; `TestPredicates_Closed` reads the package's `Pred` constants from the source by go/ast so the list and the declarations cannot drift, and `TestPredicates_EveryStoreEdgeListed` (graphtest) checks every edge the three producers write (pylite over the specimen, gopg over the shapes fixture, `knowledge.Records`). `steer.Finding` gains `Source{Kind, ID, Tier}` (kinds graph, knowledge, spec, record, proposer; ID a global id; Tier the store's word as a string, so steer imports nothing of `internal/graph`) and `Unit` (a global unit id); `Finding.Check` refuses a source kind outside the five, a source id or a unit that is not a global id, and a unit of another node kind. Intentional change: the ten line kinds (unit, reuse, consumer, decision, lesson, conflict, obligation, boundary, drift, flow) are `steer.Kind` constants with `GraphKinds()` and `Compliance(kind) ([]predicate, bool)` (opened_unit; called_symbol; edited_or_verified; paths_untouched then symbol_present; lesson_own; resolved_by_stop; met; none; returned_to_scope; consumers_verified), but the brief's finding carries no kind: `Finding.Kind` is the pattern's frozen sub-kind (`pattern.KindValid`), the task-preflight pattern has none, and M1's brief is one finding over up to five lines of mixed kinds; the kinds become frozen kinds of the graph patterns when M2 delivers one finding per line. The graph brief's finding (`cmd/rashomon/graph.go graphFinding`) carries Source{graph, the global id of the node its first line is keyed on, that node's tier} and Unit (the unit reached from that node through containing, declaring or applies_to edges, `unitOf`); `graphBrief` returns `indexBrief{engine.Brief, Repo, First, Tier, Unit}`. The steer line does not yet carry Source or Unit: the line's facts are pinned by `TestFacts_JSONTagsMatchSpec` and the key-path golden, and the writer that needs them on the line is W-M5.1's; recorded as the open end of AC-X.1. `engine.Options.Lexicon map[string][]string` is declared in `brief.go` (no `options.go`: Options lives there) and `Build` reads none of it (`TestOptions_LexiconIsReserved`). `TestCore_NoAgentKeys` (`internal/graph/graphtest/contracts_test.go`, not `agentkeys_test.go`) parses every non-test Go file under `internal/graph`, `internal/verify` and `internal/learn` and fails on an import of `internal/hookio` or a string literal holding tool_input, transcript_path or hook_event_name; the baseline at 8593061d was zero (the three keys appear in two comments only), so the test is strict, not a ratchet (AC-X.2's first branch). No new read, so no disclosure change; no package-level regexp. Sweep anchors for the two new `Check` guards and `unitOf` are owed to W-M1.9. Tests: `TestEvent_Vocabulary`, `TestEvents_CoverHooks`, `TestPredicates_Closed`, `TestPredicates_EveryStoreEdgeListed`, `TestCore_NoAgentKeys`, `TestFinding_Schema`, `TestOptions_LexiconIsReserved`, `TestGraphFinding_CarriesSourceAndUnit`; the whole packages events, graph/..., hookio, steer/..., install and cmd/rashomon and the whole acceptance package green through the slot. Drift found and repaired here (commit 502542f4, separate from the code commit a72b10db): the whole acceptance package ran for the first time in any M1 gate and was red on three pre-existing tests. `TestPW1b_TheWriterIsDisclosedWhereThePersonConsents` and `TestPW1b2_TheReadIsDisclosed` read the last screen paragraph that names `.pgraph`, and W-M1.4's graph index paragraph came after the plugin's pgraph paragraph from c9cef2b2 on, so the two tests read the wrong paragraph; the graph paragraph now precedes it with no words changed. `TestH113_` (fixture hygiene) flagged the pgraph sidecars the real pgraph wrote for the snapshot (W-M1.2, 1cf8f138) and shapes (W-M1.3, d799522e) fixtures, whose digests are the vouch chain the tests read whole, so each sidecar is excused by exact path in `h113Contracts` with its reason (D156's allow list covers a secret shape only; a scrubbed digest is a refused fixture); the harness of W-M1.0 (402fef33) tripped the same scan on a toolchain module path the scanner read as an email, on the consumers file's tab-separated format line, and on `pins.env` (an environment file is refused outright), so the README line and the format line are prose and the pins file is `test/live/autosteer/t1/pins.txt`, sourced by run.sh and the judges, and excused by path for its sha lines. The rename and the two prose edits are pre-registration adjustments before any T1 run (section 7.4's rule): no pin, cap, prompt, judge rule or fixture byte changed. Both fail above 300 ms (AC-M1.2) and run under RASHOMON_K7=1 only; the prompt is the frozen `test/live/autosteer/t1/prompt.txt` (RASHOMON_K7_PROMPT overrides) and the index the one above the module root (RASHOMON_K7_DIR overrides). The synthetic row (`TestK7_HookPathLatency`, 50,000 records, 18,450 nodes, 18.5 MB) read total p95 232 ms on the same run, so this repository's graph is under half the synthetic's size and cost. hooks.json timeouts unchanged (its diff against main's 69fac961 is empty; UserPromptSubmit 10 s). Budgets row `recap.pending.graph8MiB` (`test/perf/budgets.json` rss_cases, gates from W-M1.8; the case in `test/perf/graph_prompt_perf_test.go` under the perf tag): the recap pending entry, the prompt hook, over a project whose `.rashomon/graph.jsonl` is a `graphtest.Synthetic` index of at least 8 MiB (8,888 nodes, 17,715 edges, 8.9 MB when measured), a premise run that requires a graph line in the steer log (the slot's source_digest key, which only the graph brief writes) and a control run with `.rashomon` moved away: the median of nine over three batches 39.11, 39.02 and 39.39 MiB against 8.75 to 8.77 MiB with nothing to read, so the read adds about 30.3 to 30.6 MiB; lines darwin/arm64 42 and linux/amd64 41.5 by the pgraph rows' derivation, PROVISIONAL until anchored from CI with scripts/reanchor; `TestDEL7_EveryEntryHasABudgetRow` admits W-M1.8 as a recap case's gates_from, and the control log line says nothing to read rather than .pgraph (nothing parses it). The R2 finding: on a repository this size latency is a third of the budget and memory is the cost. The prompt hook's peak RSS goes from under 9 MiB to about 39 MiB, four and a half times, because `graph.Load` keeps every node and edge with its attribute map, where the platform read's keepNamed decode adds 2 MiB for a 3 MiB log. Intent beyond the text: W-M6.6's conditional binary cache answers the wrong question for this; the lever is a store that decodes less at the prompt (attributes the brief never reads left on disk, or a lazy attribute decode), and that is a design question for the M2 engine detail, not acted on here; the per-tool-call summed-peak budget (32 MiB) does not govern the prompt hook, and no budget for a prompt hook's peak exists beyond this row. `graphtest.Synthetic` (the former local synthetic of k7_test.go, exported) is the one fixture the K7 row and the RSS case share, so the two measure one shape. Defect found at task open and repaired here, W-M1.4's: `headCommit` read a worktree's branch ref under the worktree's git directory, where git keeps only HEAD, so every index built in a worktree carried commit C1 (this worktree's index did, read before the fix); `baseline.GitDirs(root) (gitDir, commonDir, ok)` resolves the .git pointer (relative or absolute) and the commondir file once for both `originURL` and `headCommit`, HEAD from the git directory, refs and packed-refs from the common one (`TestGitDirs_ResolvesThePointerAndTheCommonDir`; `TestHeadCommit_ReadsThroughAWorktree`: main repository, worktree with a packed ref, detached HEAD, no repository). T1's arm G indexes in a scratch worktree, so without this its header would have said C1. No new read, so no disclosure change. Tests: the two K7 tests, `TestGitDirs_`, `TestHeadCommit_`, `TestRES28_MaxRSSFollowsThePayloadBudget/recap.pending.graph8MiB` (perf tag), `TestRES28j_`, `TestRES28h_` and `TestDEL7_` over the new row; the whole packages baseline, graph/..., cmd/rashomon, test/perf, budget and disclose and the whole acceptance package green through the slot.

**W-M1.9 as built (2026-11-05 04:2x, principal's call pending the owner's word).** Defect found at task open and repaired here, W-M1.4's, under D160 (the owner's standing ruling, not a new question): the prompt hook wrote `engine.Brief.Explain()` under the store's graph directory, and Explain prints the prompt itself, every stemmed token with its weight and, on each scope row, the prompt words it matched, so every prompt that reached a brief was stored in clear under the install store (the red run reproduced it: the prompt line, three token forms and fourteen rows of matched words in the file). `Brief.Evidence()` (new) is Explain with nothing of the prompt: the scorer and the token count, the rev and elapsed line, the scope rows with their ids, kinds, scores and the count of words matched, and the rank, select and render tables unchanged; `writeGraphEvidence` writes it; `Explain` stays for the typed `rashomon graph brief` verb, where the prompt is the person's own argument on their own terminal. The disclosure paragraph's last sentence now says the selection is written "with no word of the prompt" (W-M1.4's paragraph had said "with the prompt words it matched": disclosed, and wrong under D160). `TestEvidence_HoldsNoWordOfThePrompt` builds the specimen brief for "purging the sessions" and asserts Explain carries the prompt and its tokens while Evidence carries neither the raw word, the prompt line, a weighted token nor any row's matched words, and still every table; `TestGraphBrief_EvidenceFileHoldsNoWordOfThePrompt` reads the file the hook path writes and asserts the same, plus the brief and its first node. Truth table (D161): PF-PL2 cites `pgraph/symbols.go Symbols`, `platform.ReadSnapshot`, `gopg.Resolver.Extract` and `graphBrief`, gains a UserPromptSubmit integration row through `cmd/rashomon/graph.go`, and its missing item is now the out-of-band index (no overlay until W-M2.5); PF-PL7's stale note (W-DEBT.4) now names `internal/platform/callers.go` `Locate` and `Callers` as the package-level PROVEN callers read behind the prompt note's direct-caller count, status still none, missing the two numbers and reverse reachability, which are T1's task; PF-PL11 waits for T1's PR. Sweep anchors (`test/mutation/mutants/WM-1.9_graph-guards.py`, fourteen rows): the two `Finding.check` guards (W-M1.7); `unitOf`'s unit return, four-node bound and applies_to fallback (W-M1.7), under the new `TestUnitOf_WalksContainingDeclaringAndAppliesToEdges`, which pins that a node three hops from its unit is found and one four hops away is not; `headCommit`'s packed-ref and common-directory reads and `GitDirs`'s pointer, relative join and commondir (W-M1.8); the two D160 guards; the importer's clause refusal and applies_to drop (W-M1.6). Drift found and repaired here: the sweep's static check, never run in an M1 gate, was red on seven rows in five other files, none this task's: the 11-04 merge resolution (70597cf0) wrote literal tab and newline bytes into the two preflight anchors of `LRN-17_learn-deliver.py` and `PF-1_preflight.py` (Python syntax errors, so each file declared no mutant), the merge left `DONE1_done-note.py` behind the branch's `!graphed` and `GS-1_graph-slot.py` behind main's `!done` on the same guard, and W-M1.1's six-line walk file moved `PW1b-2_stop-platform.py`'s walk anchor; the check now passes over 5,253 rows in 453 files. The live sweep over the new file and the five repaired ones judged 152 rows: every row of this task's file and of LRN-17 killed, 147 in all, and five undetected, every one in a repaired file and none this task's: DONE1's two `TakeUnverified` guards (the once-only take and the session's own flag) are shadowed in the hook path, where promptNotes takes only after recap.Turn has applied the same two checks, so the acceptance tests cannot see them, and they are judged now by `TestDONE1g_`, which observes the take directly; GS-6's refused-line row dropped the loop index's only reader, so its build broke (`_ = i` keeps it); PF-1's withheld-count row has been unobserved since TOKEN-BUDGET1 took the count off the model's line, so `TestPF1e_` now asserts the count on `Resolve` (two withheld, none named); PW1b-2's refused-walk row keyed on a four-line file that W-M1.1's symbols line made five, so its break matched nothing, and it keys on the missing log line now. The recap and intent files are identical to main and GS-1 is the branch's own, so the first four are survivors main has too (CI's full sweep is on demand only), found here because the merge's anchor repairs put their files in this sweep; the rerun over the four repaired files killed all 88, so the 152 rows of the six files are all killed between the two runs. The full sweep over `internal/graph` stays the M1 gate (W-DEBT.6). **W-M1.10 pilot read (2026-11-05 10:5x, n=3 per arm, nine runs, read only; no engine, knowledge, prompt, judge, fixture or pin byte changed after any run).** The nine runs (U, R, G three times, serial through the one logged-in configuration directory, Sonnet, 80 turns, 10 USD cap per run) ran 06:53 to 10:41 PST on 5f851724 trees under caffeinate; every judge ran after the ninth. Result: the pilot is invalid as an attribution experiment and stands as nine readings of the agent alone. Defect one (the harness, W-M1.0's; ledger B448): every note the R and G arms built was suppressed, 64 of 64 (r1 11, g1 9, r2 10, g2 6, r3 15, g3 13; steer.ndjson state suppressed on every row; a1.json's A1c read zero lines in all six), because Claude Code 2.1.280 sets CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=sdk-cli for the hooks of a `claude -p` session (hook-env.txt in every R and G run), `harness.detect` maps sdk-cli to the sdk surface, which has no in-band mirror (`internal/steer/notice.go`), so the task-preflight brief and the claim-vs-record line were shadowed by no_mirror and the blast-radius and masked-status notes by unverified_channel. W-M1.0's hook.sh exports the cli entrypoint only when the variable is unset, and it is never unset under `claude -p`; the 11-04 harness's hook.sh exported it unconditionally, which is the parity the README promises (a session that behaves as one launched from a terminal). The G brief itself was built at the prompt in 90 to 93 ms (178 ms once; hook-ms.log) and in t1-g1 named `Callers` and `Locate` on its first line: the intelligence reached the channel and the channel's surface rule, working as written for an unattended sdk session, kept it from the agent; the fix is the harness's, not the rule's. The smoke runs before the pilot drove the arm binary by replay and never through `claude -p`, so no run exercised the surface before the nine paid ones. Defect two (the batch environment, outside the repository; B449): the batch driver's PATH held no go binary, so a3's build, a6's vet and tests and a7's go test exited 127 on all nine runs (A3, A4, A5 unparsed; A6 false; A7 red everywhere); a2 prepends RK_GO_TOOLCHAIN_BIN for its sync and stands; a6's gofmt check printed pass with gofmt absent, because it reads the command's output and not its exit status (B450). The judge bytes at 158c7a29 are unchanged, so a re-judge of the nine existing trees under a PATH that holds go is the pre-registered judge's first real run, owed before any reading of A3 to A7 (script /tmp/rk-autosteer/t1-rejudge.sh, written 10:48 PST and not run; its aborted launch left a marker line and no row at line 10 of t1-rows.txt). What stands: A1a, files opened before the first edit, u1 4/4, r1 3/4, g1 3/4, u2 2/4, r2 3/4, g2 3/4, u3 3/4, r3 4/4, g3 3/4 (callers.go and the three consumers; laststop.go the usual miss); A1b false in all nine (no plan text named `Callers` and a consumer before the first edit); A2 true in all nine (every run added functions in `internal/platform` with a PROVEN caller in `cmd/rashomon` and duplicated no reader), so A2 does not discriminate on this prompt, whose words "platform read" name the unit, and T1b's judge design must say so; A8: cost U 4.669, 4.375, 4.043 (mean 4.362), R 4.259, 3.835, 3.993 (mean 4.029), G 4.349, 4.925, 4.587 (mean 4.620) USD; seven of nine ended at the turn cap (error_max_turns at 81 turns), t1-g1 ended at 96 turns and t1-r3 at 10 (success by the result json; A7 names t1-g1's done as unbacked); durations 5 to 37 minutes. Stop rule: every arm's mean is above 2.50, so n=10 is closed under ruling 12 and waits for the owner's word; the meter read 16 percent weekly at the batch's end. No lift language applies: nothing was steered. T1b (its own pre-registration under section 7.4's rule, no run before the owner's word) carries: hook.sh exporting the cli entrypoint unconditionally; judge.sh refusing to start without go on PATH; a6's gofmt judged by exit status; the batch PATH with go; a channel smoke before any paid run (one `claude -p` prompt through the R hooks, then a steer.ndjson row with state sent); and the owner's ruling on the turn cap, which seven of nine runs hit, so A3 to A7 would judge partial work at 80 (recommendation: 150 turns and a 15 USD cap, measured at n=3 first). Intent beyond the text: a pilot's first job is to prove the channel, and this one proved it closed; the cheapest instrument, one delivered note before the first paid run, is the one T1b adds.

**W-M1.10 re-judge and T1b pre-registration (2026-11-05 14:1x, no engine, knowledge, prompt, judge, fixture or T1 pin byte changed; code 40b917ba).** Re-judge: the nine pilot trees were judged again with the T1 judge's bytes at 158c7a29 (git archive into /tmp/rk-autosteer/judge-158c7a29, digests checked before the pass) under a PATH that holds go1.27.3, through the slot, 15 minutes, zero API cost; each run's pilot judge/ is preserved as judge-pilot/, the rows went to a separate file (docs-out/plans/evidence-2026-11-05/rejudge/), and the pilot's nine rows are unchanged. Readings: A3 neither 9/9 (no renderer of any arm's binary printed a line naming an oracle symbol at PostToolUse or Stop; the replay's PostToolUse blast line was delivered by all nine binaries, so the replay's channel is proven and the reading is the arms'; t1-r2, the one run that changed the blast line, prints "at least 1 proven, 1 proven or possible" for the package and names no symbol, which the prompt's "for every Go symbol" requires); A4 and A5 unparsed 9/9 (they follow from A3); A6 false 9/9, in eight runs on pl7-moved alone (gofmt, vet, LAW10, RES-2, PRD1 and the disclosure tests pass; no arm moved the truth-table row PF-PL7 off "none"), t1-r3 also on gofmt (internal/platform/symbols_test.go unformatted); A7 green 9/9 (go test over the three packages ok on every final tree); the Stop hook fired only in t1-g1, t1-r3 and t1-g3 (the six runs that ended at the turn cap had no Stop), and t1-g1's done was unbacked (claimed with go test not run after the last edit); primary (A2, A3 both, A4 match) fails 9/9. What the nine readings say: every arm produced a compiling, test-passing, partial implementation of its own shape (the surfaces split: four runs on the Stop's platform read, four on the prompt's symbols line, one on the PostToolUse blast line; none met "wherever the platform read already appears"); the one check where arm G was built to differ from U, pl7-moved (the pinned knowledge names the truth table in 18 lines and a1c counts "truth table" among its knowledge terms), failed in all three G runs because nothing was delivered. Nothing here reads the intelligence. Root cause of A1c false, verified by zero-cost replay of the frozen prompt through the G binary from the t1-g1 tree (docs-out/plans/evidence-2026-11-05/b448-replay): G, the knowledge was available (the brief built in 90 ms named Callers and Locate on line 1); A, nothing was delivered (under CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=sdk-cli the hook's stdout was 0 bytes and the steer row read suppressed no_mirror; the same replay under cli, and under sdk-cli through the corrected hook.sh, delivered 1,642 bytes of additionalContext with the row delivered); D, every agent opened two to four of the four reuse and consumer files on its own before its first edit (A1a) and read no brief. Engine reading on the brief that would have arrived (what A1c would have scored): unit false, reuse true, consumer false, knowledge 0, one of four. Why: the prompt's word "blast" collides with the existing blast-radius subsystem, so all eight seeds are blast* symbols (SeedFloor 1.0, MaxSeeds 8); the reuse target Callers is reached at depth 1 through blastPort and named on line 1; the three consumers are not among the 275 nodes reached at depth 2; the knowledge bonus lifted render.go on two LAW10 line-count lessons (B421, B439), neither in the judge's knowledge terms. Recorded as the first number T1b has to beat, not tuned (ruling 11: the prompt and the judge stand). T1b as pre-registered (test/live/autosteer/t1b/, README T1b section; held down by test/acceptance/t1b_live_harness_test.go, four tests written before the fix, three red on the pilot's harness, all green after): hook.sh exports CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=cli always, after recording what Claude Code set in hook-env.txt (B448); t1b/judge/judge.sh refuses, exit 3 and no row, without go, gofmt, python3, perl and git on PATH (B449); t1b/judge/a6.sh reads gofmt's exit status and output together (B450); run.sh takes RK_SLICE, RK_PROMPT_FILE and RK_MODEL and records slice, model and prompt in the run directory; t1b/batch.sh is the versioned driver (RK_T1B_GO=yes only on the owner's word; the toolchain bin first on PATH; every tool checked; the smoke first; each run sanity-checked, channel-checked by t1b/channel.py and judged as it ends; stops on any harness fault); t1b/smoke.sh is the channel smoke (one haiku turn through the G hooks on smoke-prompt.txt, about 0.02 USD) that asserts the prompt hook's additionalContext and its hook_additional_context attachment in the transcript; the T1 slice's digests are frozen in TestT1B_TheT1SliceIsFrozen. T1b's pins equal T1's (tree, binaries, prompt, caps, knowledge digests); the turn cap stays 80 so T1b rows compare with the pilot's nine, and raising it is the owner's call (the A7 readings say the trees at the cap compiled and passed; the A3 readings say the work was partial). Pre-registration adjustment under section 7.4 (2026-11-05 15:5x, the owner's word, before any T1b run; one pins commit): T1_MAX_TURNS 150 and T1_BUDGET_USD 15 in t1b/pins.txt, because at 80 every pilot arm was partial and the primary outcome could not vary; A1a and A1b stay comparable with the pilot, A3 to A8 are read against the cap difference; expected 6 to 8 USD per run, about 55 to 70 USD for nine, up to 6 h serial through the one logged-in directory; the nine pilot trees kept for a blinded A3 read; the batch word given with the meter at 19 percent (ts 1793921881 and 1793921906); n=10 stays closed under ruling 12. Driver repair under section 7.4 after the first triple (2026-11-05 18:0x, B453/B454; one batch.sh commit, no pin, prompt, judge, fixture, knowledge or hook byte moved): the batch stops before the channel check and the judge on a result with is_error true, num_turns under 2 or a limit text, and logs the configuration directory's account beside the meter, because t1b-g1 died at turn 1 under HTTP 429 seven_day on the alpha account (karthik.nerella@altrace.io, a different account from the meter file's) and was judged as a row; that row is void and the name t1b-g1 is never rerun. Whether the remaining runs continue as T1b under the same pins or open T1c is the owner's ruling, owed before any further run. Ruling and relaunch (2026-11-05 18:5x; the owner re-issued the work order 18:4x and chose the meter file's account; the blueprint session agreed on the order 18:5x): the remaining runs continue as T1b under the same pins by the order's failure path (ledger row, harness repair, pre-registration commit, another ask), through a fresh configuration directory logged into the meter file's account, whose accountUuid is checked against ~/.claude's before launch so the two meter readings gate the batch; order RK_T1B_ORDER="G:t1b-g2 U:t1b-u2 R:t1b-r2 G:t1b-g3 U:t1b-u3 R:t1b-r3 G:t1b-g4", an intentional change from the pre-registered U R G start because u1 and r1 exist and g1 is void, so every stopping point leaves the arms at equal n (1,1,1 after one run, 2,2,2 after four, 3,3,3 after seven) with the period-3 interleave kept; t1b-g1 is carried as VOID, excluded from every tally and mean by the read (is_error rows skipped), and the rows file is never edited by hand. Gate: gofmt clean, go vet ./test/acceptance 0, TestT1B_ 4 of 4, TestH115_ 6 of 6, TestRubricLAW10_ 5 of 5 through the slot; nothing pushed. Owed before any paid run: the owner's word on the T1b batch (nine runs at the pilot's 3.8 to 4.9 USD, about 40 USD on the meter, plus the smoke; the meter read 18 percent weekly at ts 1793914864 and 1793915864, two readings agreeing), the turn cap, and n=10 (closed under ruling 12 on the pilot's means). Intent beyond the text: the pilot's value is its nine fully judged agent-alone baselines; T1b buys the first measurement of delivery, and the engine's one of four on this prompt is the number the next engine change must move.

**W-M1.12 as built (2026-11-06 13:1x, principal's call pending the owner's word; code d527ed53 on b286d82f).** Opened from the T1b read: judge A1c scored the G arm's brief 1 of 4 on every run (the reuse pass by luck: blast.go calls Callers), and the diagnosis is that the brief was a top-5 of lexical file groups with no stage computing the unit, the reuse, the consumers or the knowledge the 5.2 objective names; the words matched (platformRead, Read, Callers), the roles were missing, so ruling 9's remedy (vocabulary) did not apply to T1. Built: a Roles stage between Scope and Rank (frame.go: targets, additions, constraints and uses from the prompt's verbs, place words and use words, a condition word opening no phrase; units.go: one unit per target phrase by qualified-name coverage, claimants only (callables and files; a constant is a label), ties by kind, precision and rest-of-prompt mass, then call direction, else refused, never faked; roles.go: the target file, consumers by proven in-edges with one wrapper hop and a unit-caller fallback, reuse admitted inside the unit, by a unit member's call, or by a name holding the addition's head, ranked use, via the addition's code, add mass, in-unit, reused, mass, path; knowledge by specificity, each id mapped to the file it was found through; lines.go: role lines first, related lines after, cut at five); truth-table rows imported as requirement knowledge (PF-xx, the title with status and first missing item as the clause); UnitFact, Role and KnowledgeRequirement in the slot; the record JSON gains role, consumers and counts.consumers. Measured at zero model cost on the frozen T1b tree's index with the truth table imported: T1 by hand against A1c's substrings 4 of 4 (unit internal/platform, reuse callers.go, consumer report_platform.go, requirement PF-PL11), from 1 of 4. AC-M1.1 (TestACM11_UnitLineNamesThePreNamedUnit over test/live/autosteer/acm11/prompts.tsv): unit line right 4 of 5 (T1, PG12, HD9, ST12; PL12 names internal/verify where pgraph was pre-named); off-task lines T1 0, PG12 0, HD9 1 (internal/store/record_v5.go, the reuse), ST12 1 (internal/learn/ledger.go, the knowledge line), PL12 5 (a wrong unit makes every line off-task), so the row's second clause fails on PL12 alone and the test fails as written; the baseline had no unit line (0 of 5). The development replay (513 commit subjects of origin/main, truth the packages each commit touched): unit@1 48.9 percent from 39.4, identical over three runs of one binary, a development reading, not lift. AC-M1.2 on the same index (20,378 records, the T1 prompt, 30 runs, go1.27.3, paired against b6914ff6 in one slot window): TestK7_RashomonGraph pipeline p95 32.0 ms (base 31.2), total p95 97.2 (base 92.7); TestK7_PromptPathOnRashomon p95 107.8 ms (base 100.8); both under 300; the synthetic 50K-record reading (TestK7_HookPathLatency, no assertion) pipeline p95 178 ms from 72 and total p95 303 to 312 from 192 to 220, at the 300 line, because the corpus scan tokenizes every node per prompt: a per-rev token cache is the next row before any larger index. Gate under go1.27.3 through the slot: gofmt clean, go vet ./... clean, internal/graph/engine, internal/steer/render/slot, internal/steer/render, internal/steer, cmd/rashomon, internal/graph/knowledge, internal/graph/index, internal/hook and test/acceptance (LAW10 with roles.go at 491 code lines, RES-2, TRU2c, the no-language-literal guard) all ok. Three defects caught and fixed before close, ledger B455 to B457 (reuse precision counted tokens the request lacked; a knowledge line named the record's first file instead of the file it was found through; set sums in map order made the unit line nondeterministic, 9 of 513 replay rows flipping between two runs of one binary). PL12's miss is read, not patched: "no tracked build configuration covers" sits in a relative clause the frame does not open and "configuration" does not stem to pgraph's "config"; the relative-clause rule and the stem are T1c rows, as are the one-token reuse claims (PG12 settingsEdit through "edit", ST12 store.Open and ledger.go through "open", PL12 isCase through "case"). For the owner: the pre-named units and allow lists in prompts.tsv (PL12 pgraph or internal/verify; HD9's internal/store; ST12's internal/learn) were written before the runs and not moved after; AC-M1.1's second clause counts a wrong unit as five off-task lines, so it is a 5-of-5 gate in effect, and the owner rules whether M1 closes on 4 of 5 with PL12 recorded or the clause reads "among briefs whose unit line is right"; ruling 9's premise is wrong for T1 and right for PL12 only (note docs-out/research-2026-11-06-autosteer-m1-roles.md, sections 3, 5, 6). The T1b trees are untouched; this engine opens T1c (7.4), whose pre-registration waits for the owner's word and the turn-cap ruling.

**W-M1.12 second opinion and T1c pre-registration (2026-11-06 13:2x, proposed; code unchanged at d527ed53; T1c opens on the owner's word).** The owner sent the four owed rulings to the read-only reviewer session; its answer was checked line by line against the tree before anything here was written. PL12's pre-named unit was wrong, not the engine: pgraph/doc.go lists blast radius and the untracked-configuration warning in one sentence as later slices (lines 31-32) and PF-PL7 cites the same file, yet T1's unit is internal/platform; pgraph imports nothing from rashomon and rashomon nothing from pgraph (doc.go 5-6); the prompt's surface, "wherever the platform obligations already appear", is rashomon's (internal/verify/platform.go platformObligations, cmd/rashomon/report_ledger.go, internal/recap/platform.go platformLines); the data the warning needs already crosses the boundary (every fact carries its Config, pgraph/ingest.go; internal/platform/decode.go reads it; pgraph.digests lists one line per Go file the sync read, digests.go 35; WalkDigest is a superset of what a sync reads, walk.go 20-21); and verify/platform.go 42-43 names the defect itself, a stale package and a new one judged alike. Reading proposed to the owner: PL12's unit is internal/verify, file internal/verify/platform.go, reuse internal/platform/digests.go and decode.go, consumers report_ledger.go and recap platformLines, requirements PF-PL12 and PF-PL11. Re-scored at zero cost on d527ed53 with a scratch copy of prompts.tsv carrying that truth plus internal/store/record_v5.go on HD9's allow list (the committed file is untouched): unit line 5 of 5; off-task PL12 2 (internal/brief/brief.go as the consumer, internal/shape/comsub.go isCase as the reuse through the test word "case"), ST12 1 (internal/learn/ledger.go, genuinely off task), the rest 0; clause 2 still fails on PL12, and that is the true signal: M1 stays open on the role lines, not the unit line. The judge was weaker than T1's: AC-M1.1 reads the unit line and off-task paths only, so PL12's roles (1 of 4 in A1c's shape) passed the units clause; PF-PL12, whose title "Untracked build-configuration warning" is the graph's best match for the prompt, appears nowhere in PL12's 2,170 candidates because knowledge text is outside the scope corpus (scope.go 251) and "untrack" weighs 0. The 262 replay misses classified by cause (scratch classifier under a build tag, never committed; /tmp/rk-autosteer/acm11/out/replay-causes2.txt): A 27, an identifier or scope tag in the subject names the truth unit (orderRead, classifyWith, "fix(verify)"); B 214, a subject word is among the truth unit's names and the ladder settled elsewhere (171 wrong claims, 123 of them partial; 43 refusals); C 0, no word names any code; D 21, the words name code and none of it in the truth. Over all 513 rows by claim class: partial claims right 153 of 302, full 66 of 114, refused-below-floor 12 of 47 through the first-line fallback. Sub-classified at the reviewer's caution that class B is a residual bucket, not a cause (replay-causes3.txt, 13:3x): the truth unit sits somewhere in the ladder's picks in 236 of 262 misses; same class and out-ranked by coverage 135, out-ranked by claim class 45 (41 of them full over partial), refused with the truth lower in the picks 32, refused with the truth as the refused top pick 13, the truth made no claim on the target phrase 25, a tie the other way 11, no claim 1. The coverage losses favour size: the winner is the larger unit in 99 of the 135 and 32 of the 45, at a median 2.1 times the truth's node count (internal/verify, 788 nodes, wins 53 of those rows and is the truth in 14; internal/disclose, 73 nodes, is the truth in 36 and never wins), because a unit's claim is the union of every name it declares and a large union covers a random word by chance. R1's anchor markers appear in 0 of 513 subjects and R2's clause words in 46 of 262 misses, so R1 and R2 are measured on AC-M1.1's prompts and the replay's target is the size-normalized claim (W-M1.18). Coverage class is a weak signal on this repo: full claims are right 66 of 114, partial 153 of 302. Ledger B460. Measured the same afternoon (13:4x) before any engine change, on a frozen split by the row id's last hex digit: strict best-member scoring refuses 422 of 513 rows at the floor, because 456 of 513 subjects parse no target and the whole subject (mean 6.5 tokens) is the phrase; best-member ranking over the union floor is +5 and -2 on the halves; union over 1+ln(1+vocabulary) is +9 and +8 with internal/verify's rows kept; vocab^0.25, sqrt and linear lose 0 to 66 rows per half. The size preference is mostly a legitimate prior, so the replay's headroom is not in re-weighting the coverage signal; W-M1.18 was re-aimed at the fallback phrase, then withdrawn at the reviewer's objection (13:5x): a rule tuned on commit-subject grammar would raise the replay without serving a prompt anyone types. A prompt replay built from the local transcripts (owner-typed rows 3, peer work orders 112) parses a target in 94 of 112 rows and resolves the unit in 23 of 112; the ln form is -5/-2 there and a strongest-claim-across-phrases arm is -1 on both sets. The commit replay is a regression guard, the ln form is held, and the instrument is W-M1.18's deliverable (ledger B461). The reviewer's named-path rule (R0 for paths) measured on the same set: 32 of 112 against the engine's 23, the largest single gain of the day, folded into W-M1.13. Product reading, recorded for the owner's ruling: on a work order that names its paths the unit line is settled by the text, and the brief's value is the four lines after it (consumers, reuse and knowledge the order did not mention, and a rule the order would break); unit resolution matters for short human prompts, and a public file-localization set (SWE-bench gold patches, Loc-Bench, Multi-SWE-bench if its Go coverage holds) is the reproducible number the strategy note lacks. The ladder's own change: a target word no declared name holds weighs at the scorer's weight when the tree mentions it, else at the mean of the known words (ledger B462: at one build internal/gateinfer took "Fix the CI workflow so the perf-tagged tests run on linux only" through its name fromCI because the unknown words were outside the floor; the edge set caught it). **W-M1.29 built and not merged (2026-11-08 00:3x to 00:5x; code 2e4d72a1 on a local branch, never pushed; M1's acceptance read ran at 28ca31b9, whose engine code is E's).** The rule: when no file outside the unit calls or references a target symbol or any unit symbol by name, the consumer role reads the files that may call a unit symbol (pgraph's ALGORITHMIC dispatch through an interface). The prediction registered in its commit: on the development half ST12, E7, N3 and A4 move from empty to one of internal/hookio/stream.go, internal/jsonscan/stream_window.go and internal/store/latestrecord.go, M9 stays wrong, and no other row moves. A5, B6, JP4 and O4 on the acceptance half moved the same way: consumers 134 to 142 of 160, reuse 114 to 113 of 140. The role-level prediction missed. `roleLines` (lines.go) orders a brief as the unit, the target, then reuse[i], consumer[i] and knowledge[i] in rounds, cut at five lines; the new consumer took a round-one slot, and all eight briefs were full, so each lost its last line: reuse[1] on ST12, A4 and A5 (on A4 the reuse truth internal/harness/detect.go) and knowledge[2] on B6, E7, JP4, N3 and O4 (requirement lines PF-HD5, PF-HD15 twice and PF-HD12 twice; no lesson or decision line was cut). Line accounting, each class of line under its own standard. The eight added consumer lines are judged at run time in the tree: internal/hookio/stream.go on five rows is a real consumer (intake.go:181 passes countingReader to hookio.IntakeStream, which reads it); internal/jsonscan/stream_window.go on two rows reads hookio's objectReader, one hop away; internal/store/latestrecord.go on N3 wraps a file with its own countingReader and is false at run time, and it is that brief's consumer line because the rank orders scope score before path (the commit's "ranks third on path" is wrong). The removed lines are judged against the derived truths the judge reads, with no run-time check: six true (one reuse, five requirement) and two wrong reuse lines. Net, counting one hop as not true: one fewer true line and one more false; counting it true: one more true and one fewer false. Not merged, for three reasons. The judged gain of seven rows is coverage the judge counts, paid for with lines it cannot see. On cmd/rashomon's rows the consumer truths were derived from the same may-call relation the rule reads (12 of the 41 truth files of the five rows the commit names, every one a file that may call the unit's readers through io.Reader), so the judge scores the line right by construction and only the run-time read is independent. And a round-one line with no "possible" basis word costs true requirement lines and states a guess as a proven consumer. An evidence order, a may-call consumer after every proven line, is the principled form and changes no row in hand, since every brief the rule reached was full; it was not built before the acceptance read. Carried to M2: a may-call consumer, labelled as possible, fills only spare budget, and its precision is judged at run time, never against a may-call-derived truth (`classes2.tsv`'s cmd/rashomon consumer cells carry the same derivation). Decided at 00:49, before the acceptance read started at 00:57.

M1 therefore stops claiming a unit from a prose description and claims instead that it states nothing false there. Gate shape 3 for AC-M1.5, read once on `classes3.tsv` at the engine W-M1.38 freezes:
- A row is a naming row when its class is named-name, named-path, pasted-error or knowledge-id, or its prompt holds a truth unit's path, last path segment or a target file's name (case-folded, bounded by non-alphanumerics, a path as a substring: the rule that splits the development files 81, 62 and 17, and 58, 84 and 18); a no-unit row when its unit truth is '-'; otherwise a prose row.
- Unit verdict: a naming row and a no-unit row as shape 2 (the brief's unit a truth; no unit line where the truth is '-'); a prose row is right when the brief states no wrong unit: no unit line, a stated unit that is a truth, or a candidate pair holding a truth.
- Per class, all sixteen, the unit verdict right in 7 of 10. Reuse, consumers and knowledge are judged on the rows whose brief unit is a truth (the role lines of a wrong or withheld unit are that unit's error, counted once): per class right in 7 of 10 of the rows judged, rounded up, where at least three are judged, and over the 160 rows each kind right in 7 of 10 of the rows judged.
- Gate B over the 160 rows: stated unit lines right in at least 0.8 of those stated; candidate lines holding a truth in at least 0.5 of those delivered (W-M1.38's floors).
- Printed beside the gate, not gated: per class the prose rows and the share the brief withheld; naming-row unit right pooled.
Shape 2's read on `classes2.tsv` stands as not met. A unit from a prose description is not claimed by M1; the numbers above are where that work starts. W-M1.38's sentence that the judge does not change is superseded for prose rows by this shape; its floors stand.

**Gate shape 3 built in the judge; W-M1.38's role-line bands (2026-11-08 02:2x, before any `classes3.tsv` row exists).** The judge is `internal/graph/engine/acm15_shape3_test.go` at 96cc5b40, and `TestACM15_NamingSplit` pins the naming rule to the counts above (with the four naming classes, 103, 40 and 17, and 80, 62 and 18). Two readings of the registered words are fixed in it. A brief that withholds its unit line and keeps a role line claims that line's unit (the unit of its first line, shape 2's rule), so dropping the unit line alone never makes a prose row right; at 28ca31b9 none of the 37 briefs without a unit line keeps a line, so no reading moves. A candidate is a unit line that renders its runner-up, read from the line's band once W-M1.38 adds one. A brief that withholds on a prose row is right by construction, so the prose-row count measures how much the brief withholds and not whether it finds the unit; the numbers that carry evidence are naming-row unit right, gate B, and the prose rows where the brief states a unit. Development readings at the engine of 28ca31b9, matched cell for cell by an independent tally of the same briefs: on `classes2.tsv` every gate is met on 3 of 16 classes and the unit gate on 6; prose rows right 11 of 62 with none withheld; naming rows 59 of 80; stated unit lines right 48 of 71 and candidate lines holding a truth 21 of 52, both under gate B; reuse given the unit 44 of 66. On `classes.tsv`, the set the engine was worked on: 11 of 16 and 16 of 16, prose 31 of 40, naming 99 of 103, stated 82 of 89, candidate 42 of 49. The same engine and judge on the two files are the overfit, measured. W-M1.38's role-line bands are read per line kind under the basis of the unit line it hangs off, and never above that unit line's band: a role line is right only when its unit is, and the per-kind pooled precision mixes evidence of different strength (target lines are right 36 of 37 under a unit named in the request and 37 of 94 under a unit by word coverage). This unit of reading was chosen after that table was printed and is recorded as a post-hoc refinement; the floors stay 0.8 and 0.5 on delivered precision pooled over the two development files. Pooled at 28ca31b9's engine: under a named unit the target, consumer and knowledge lines are stated and the reuse lines withheld (0 of 6); under a unit by its name the consumer and knowledge lines are candidates and the target and reuse lines withheld; under word coverage the consumer lines are candidates and the target, reuse and knowledge lines (75 of 156) withheld. A withheld role line on a row whose unit is a truth is judged wrong where its truth is not '-': the floors' cost shows in the role gates and is not engineered around.

**W-M1.38 as built; the judge reads the band; development readings and the names-no-unit change (2026-11-08 03:4x, before any `classes3.tsv` row exists).** Code 291803ea, the W-M1.38 commit 3df223b9 picked onto this branch (non-test sources identical; the judge files and docs differ); judge bc4b8190. **AC-M1.5 re-specified from VISION, before any `classes4.tsv` row exists (2026-11-08 12:2x PST; a post-hoc change to what the brief owes and to how AC-M1.1 is judged, recorded as one).** VISION's task-intelligence outcome (B) asks the engine to "avoid false-positive guidance"; the close3 read shows where the false guidance comes from, and the re-specification makes the brief owe only what the engine can state truly. Every figure below is a simulation over the frozen d4f5d05e briefs in close3's dumps, by a simulator that reproduces the judge exactly when no rule is applied (unit right 144, 95 and 92; classes at the unit gate 16, 7 and 6). `classes2.tsv` is the design file; `classes3.tsv` figures are in-sample after its one read.
- Where the false guidance comes from. Unit lines on the two untuned files, split without truths by whether the request names the claimed unit or its runner-up (path, or last segment as a bounded word): named basis 66 of 76 right (0.87); by the unit's name, self-named 24 of 45, not named 2 of 7; by word coverage, self-named 9 of 14, not named 26 of 92 (0.28). By the units ladder's form: a qualified claim covering the whole target phrase (the unit's path covers part of it and one of its names the rest, T1's form) 4 of 5 (13 of 13 on `classes.tsv`); one name covering a one-token phrase 6 of 31; every other coverage form under 0.5 over the two files together; a claim by the unit's name in a target phrase of at most two tokens 8 of 11, in a longer phrase 18 of 41. No margin on the ladder's scores separates right from wrong, and T1's unit wins on class at equal coverage (5.72 of 5.72, four units tied), so no score-margin rule keeps it.
- Calibration. The bands in `bands.go` were read over `classes.tsv`, the set the engine was tuned on, and `classes2.tsv`; their cells overstate. Bands are read on files the engine was not tuned on.
- Knowledge. A cited record's `applies_to` names a truth unit on 12 of the 20 knowledge-id rows of the two untuned files; the engine claims from the record on few of them.
- The gate's power. Sixteen per-class gates at 7 of 10 pass together with 0.13 at a per-row accuracy of 0.80, 0.44 at 0.85, 0.81 at 0.90 (classes independent). A fresh set is worth authoring only when both development files show every class at 8 of 10 or better.
The re-specification:
1. A unit is claimed only from what the request names (unit rule E5): the basis is named (a path, a file, a symbol, a knowledge id, a pasted error); or a cited record's `applies_to` names the unit; or the ladder's claim is qualified and covers the whole target phrase; or the claim is by the unit's name in a target phrase of at most two tokens. Any other claim becomes "not known: the unit to change" in place and takes its role lines with it, the mechanism d4f5d05e ships.
2. AC-M1.1 is judged as gate shape 3 judges: the pre-named unit is owed on a prompt that names it (its path, last segment or target file name, shape 3's definition), and elsewhere a brief is right when it states no wrong unit; four of five per file; the basis and off-task clauses unchanged. As written, AC-M1.1 owes a unit line on prompts that name no unit, which shape 3 refuses; both rows read the same brief and owe the same thing. At d4f5d05e the change moves one row, E1, in both files: it states `internal/store` as a candidate with the truth `internal/safe` as its runner-up, which shape 3 credits on a prose row, so prompts read 10 of 10 and edge 48 of 54 (9 and 47 as written; the earlier wording of this sentence, that no verdict moves, was wrong and is corrected here).
3. Gate B unchanged (stated lines at least 0.8, candidates at least 0.5), read on the fresh set.
4. T1c's G arm is inert to the change only if T1's brief context keeps sha256 7641dab9cde93040, the prompt brief keeps pattern task-preflight and key graph, and every later prompt event with a new prompt id replays identical; otherwise G runs again at the new engine.
Rule 1 without the knowledge clause, simulated: `classes2.tsv` unit 120 of 160, the unit gate on 12 of 16 classes, candidates 9 of 11; `classes3.tsv` (in-sample) 125, 14 of 16, 3 of 5; `classes.tsv` 130, 14 of 16, 36 of 39; AC-M1.1 under clause 2, prompts 10 of 10 and edge 47 of 54, wrong units stated 0 and 2. On the knowledge-id rows the knowledge clause adds at most 2 rows on `classes2.tsv` and 6 on `classes3.tsv` by the cited records' links (three of the six records link several units, so the pick decides), which takes knowledge-id to at most 8 of 10 on both; its effect on rows of other classes that cite a record is not simulated. Still under the gate: feature-add 5, refactor 5 and cross-cutting 4 on `classes2.tsv`; cross-cutting 6 on `classes3.tsv`. The rule was chosen after reading both untuned files, so `classes4.tsv` is the only out-of-sample read. Prediction, registered: at rule 1 with the knowledge clause and nothing else, a fresh set authored as `classes3.tsv` was is not met, the unit gate on 12 to 15 of 16 classes with cross-cutting, refactor and feature-add the likely misses, and candidates at or over 0.5. So `classes4.tsv` is not authored at that engine. The work, each step judged on `classes2.tsv` with the other two files printed beside as in-sample: W-M1.40, rule E5 without the knowledge clause as `shippedUnitRule`, gated by clause 4, AC-M1.1 under clause 2, the replay guard reported and K7; W-M1.41, the knowledge clause, where a record linking several units claims the one the ladder ranks first among them; W-M1.42, prose recall for the classes still under the gate: the doc comment pgraph writes on every symbol (`doc`) read into the unit's fields (the C1 gap "no doc" in section 2's table), and a target phrase of one generic token refused. `classes4.tsv` is authored when both development files show every class at 8 of 10 or better, after T1c's remaining runs, at the owner's word on its count, model and cost.

**What E5 costs, recorded before it ships (2026-11-08 11:3x PST).** The cross-repo fixture's Task A ("make session expiry respect the org-level timeout override.") claims `services/auth` by word coverage of the whole phrase in the full form, not the qualified form (`TestRoles_TheUnitLineCarriesTheClaimsBasisAndRunnerUp` pins `ClassFull` and the coverage basis), and cites no record, so under E5 its unit is not known, its brief holds only the carrier and is not sent (`TestUnnamed_AUnitTheRequestDoesNotNameIsNotKnown` shows this under E4, which agrees with E5 on a full claim), and the consumer line (`jobs/purge_sessions.py`, INC-212 applies) goes with it. The 11-04 cross-repo result (steered plans name the purge job 5 of 5) was measured on an engine that sent that line; at E5 the line is sent only when the request names `services/auth` or a file in it, so that result is not evidence for the shipped engine and is not cited as its behaviour.
- Why the trade stands. On the untuned files the full form covering the whole phrase is right 4 of 8 (`classes2.tsv`) and 4 of 15 (`classes3.tsv`), and word-coverage claims on units the request does not name 26 of 92; no ladder form or margin separates Task A's claim from these. Three further truth-free sources were simulated on the rows E5 leaves without a unit: the unit its symbols' doc comments rank first (BM25), delivered when it leads the second by 1.2 to 1.5 times; a record retrieved by the request's words (top 1 to 5 decisions and lessons by BM25) whose `applies_to` names the claimed unit; and keeping a line whose unit the request names by word (R1). None moves `classes2.tsv` by more than 3 rows or 1 class, each costs rows on `classes3.tsv`, and R1 wins only on `classes.tsv`, the tuning set (16 of 16), while candidates on `classes3.tsv` fall to 0.45, under gate B. A record delivered by the request's words alone, with no unit, is in the row's knowledge truths on 10 of 147 `classes2.tsv` rows that cite no record (12 of 150 on `classes3.tsv`), so knowledge re-anchored to the request does not keep the consumer line either.
- Where the errors are. Of the 20 rows wrong under E5 in the four classes under the gate on `classes2.tsv`, 17 are rows that name a truth unit by word where the ladder ranks a neighbouring or wider unit first (`internal/store` for `internal/spend`, `internal/shape` for `internal/baseline`) or holds the right one below the whole-phrase form; 3 are kept lines that are wrong. W-M1.41 and W-M1.42 therefore change the evidence the engine ranks on, not the rule that delivers, and the cross-repo reach comes back where the unit is stated truly.
- Owed with W-M1.40: a test pinning Task A both ways at the shipped rule (no brief as written; the unit and the consumer line when the request names the service or one of its files), and `TestGS5` kept on a prompt that the shipped rule keeps, so the cross-repo consumer line stays covered end to end.

### 6.2 M2 to M7 (task level; step-level detail is written when the milestone opens, after the previous slice's evidence is read, so that measured results shape the detail rather than guesses)

| Id | Deliverable | Files | Req | AC | Depends | h | Risk |

## 7. First vertical slice: T1 on rashomon itself (and the slice sequence T2 to T6)

### 7.1 Why PF-PL7

- It is a PRD row at `none`, so the agent must build, not touch up.
- Main holds a partial implementation to reuse: `internal/platform/callers.go` (package-level PROVEN callers, `Locate`, `Callers`); a parallel implementation elsewhere is the classic miss (S2).
- Its consumers are non-local and unnamed by any prompt: `internal/verify` (`PlatformRead`, `platformObligations`), `cmd/rashomon/report_platform.go`, `cmd/rashomon/laststop.go` (all verified present at 70597cf0).
- Four conventions bind it that no file in the tree states as instructions: PF-PL11 (unknown never zero), LAW10 (100 lines per function, 500 per file), RES-2 (no package-level regexp), D161 (the truth-table row travels with the change), plus the disclosure paragraph for any new read. Verified: the repository tracks no `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` or `.claude/` directory, and the harness runs under its own `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, so the owner's global instructions are not loaded in either arm. The only way these reach the agent is the steered arm's brief (from the imported ledger and decision rows) or the agent's own reading of `docs-out`, which is outside git and therefore absent from a scratch worktree.
- It exercises the slice requirements the owner listed: ambiguous intent (no file named), architectural discovery (the owning unit), unnamed dependency and abstraction (callers.go, the three consumers), the real channel (UserPromptSubmit through the hook set), measurable planning and implementation change (A1 to A3), obligations (D161, tests), independent verification (hidden oracle, rubric tests), persistent task state (the record and the Stop verdict in the steered arm).

### 7.2 Frozen prompt (proposed; frozen by the pre-registration commit and never edited after a run)

```
Add blast radius to the platform read. For every Go symbol an edit touches, report two numbers: how many callers are proven, and how many are proven or possible. Show the numbers wherever the platform read already appears. Keep the existing tests green and add tests for the new numbers.
```

The prompt names no file, no package, no convention, and does not say "unknown".

### 7.3 Arms and environment (revised by the 11-04 review, Part B5: three arms so the agent, the existing runtime and the graph intelligence are measured apart)

| Arm | Tree | Binary and hooks | Knowledge | Runs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| U unaided | scratch worktree of `origin/main` at a pinned sha (`git worktree add`, never `cp -r`), no `.rashomon/` | none | none | 3 then 10 |
| R runtime | same sha | the main binary at 69fac961 with hooks installed into the harness config dir: record, intent preflight, gates, obligations, Stop verdict; no graph verbs, no `.rashomon/graph.json`; the tree synced with the pinned pgraph before the session, as the shipped plugin's SessionStart hook does (pre-registration adjustment 2026-11-04 23:5x, before any run: `rashomon watch` installs no pgraph hook, so without it arm R would never carry the platform read a developer has) | none | 3 then 10 |
| G AutoSteer | same sha | the branch binary from 70597cf0 plus M1, the same hooks; `pgraph sync` with the arm's own pgraph (T1_G_PGRAPH_SHA), whose sidecars its binary reads; `rashomon graph index .` with `.rashomon/graph.json` listing `.` and `pgraph` as Go units | `.rashomon/knowledge/*.json` imported from `docs-out/plans/FAILURE-LEDGER.txt` and `APPROACH-NOTES.md` at pinned digests | 3 then 10 |

Attribution per metric: runtime effect = R minus U; graph effect = G minus R; total = G minus U. The former ablation arm (G minus knowledge) runs only if G differs from R on the primary outcome and the owner asks which part. Model: Sonnet through `claude -p` with the harness's turn cap and timeout; a fresh config dir per run, logged in once before the batch. Cost: at the 2.50 USD ceiling, three arms at n=3 then n=10 are at most 39 runs, about 98 USD on the meter (ruling 12 set the per-run ceiling; the per-slice total is stated here for the record).

### 7.4 Pre-registered acceptance checks (scripts in `test/live/autosteer/t1/judge/`, committed before the first run; the agent never sees them; the judge runs on each arm's final tree and hook log)

| Id | Measures (all arms unless stated) | Source | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1a discovery by behaviour | before its first edit the agent opened `internal/platform/callers.go` and each consumer file (`internal/verify`'s PlatformRead file, `cmd/rashomon/report_platform.go`, `cmd/rashomon/laststop.go`); one row per item | the harness's tool-call log (Read, Grep, Bash with paths), the instrument the 11-04 evidence used for "read before first edit" | per item opened or not; rates compared across U, R, G |
| A1b discovery in the plan | the agent's text before its first edit names `Callers` or `callers.go` and at least one consumer file | transcript text before the first edit | present or absent |
| A1c channel (G, and R for its preflight) | the first UserPromptSubmit's additionalContext names the unit, the reuse target, a consumer and a knowledge line (PL11, LAW10, RES-2 or D161); an off-task knowledge line counts minus one | hook log, steer log seq (A), graph row ids (G) | per line; reported as the G, A, D chain beside A1a and A2, never as discovery itself |
| A2 reuse by structure | on the arm's final tree, `pgraph sync` then a judge query over `.pgraph/pgraph.log`: a new function in `internal/platform` exists whose PROVEN callers include a symbol in `cmd/rashomon` or `internal/verify`, and no new function outside `internal/platform` and `pgraph` has a `calls` edge to `readLog`, `readDigests` or `DecodeLog` | pgraph on the final tree, the same instrument for every arm | pass or fail |
| A3 consumers by behaviour | the judge replays the hook sequence, one session per oracle symbol (SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PreToolUse and PostToolUse Edit of the symbol's file in a synced copy of `pgraph/testdata/dispatch`, Stop), against a binary built from the arm's final tree with a fresh install store (pre-registration details 2026-11-04 23:5x, before any run: one symbol per session because the Stop prints at most two package lines; the fixture copy carries a CI step naming `go test ./...` and an empty `_test.go` per package, which the Stop's platform row requires on the pinned tree; neither calls an oracle symbol, oracle shapes re-verified unchanged); for the edited symbol two integers (or an integer and an unknown marker) appear on the symbol's line in both renderers the platform read has on the pinned tree, the PostToolUse blast line and the Stop's platform line (pre-registration correction 2026-11-04 23:3x: `rashomon report` has no platform section at 5f851724, so the report text is captured as information, not judged) | arm binary driven by recorded events | both, one or neither renderer; unparsed rows go to a blinded human read |
| A4 correctness (hidden oracle) | `judge/oracle.py` computes from the fixture's `.pgraph/pgraph.log`: proven fan-in = distinct subjects of `calls` to the symbol; inclusive = proven plus subjects of `may-call`; three symbols pre-registered with distinct shapes (proven only; proven and possible so the two numbers differ; none); the numbers parsed in A3 must equal the oracle | oracle script, A3 output | match, mismatch, unparsed |
| A5 unknown never zero (hidden) | a copy of the dispatch fixture adds one file importing `reflect` and calling a method by name (pgraph's fixtures hold no such boundary today, verified); the inclusive number for the affected symbol must not print as a bare zero | A3 replay on the fixture copy | pass, fail, unparsed |
| A6 architecture compliance | `gofmt -l` empty; `go vet ./...`; `TestRubricLAW10_` by name; the RES-2 test; the truth-table test with the PL7 row moved off `none` (D161); the disclosure test for any new read | arm tree through the slot | each pass or fail |
| A7 verification | the judge's `go test ./internal/platform ./internal/verify ./cmd/rashomon` on the final tree (all arms); for R and G: the Stop verdict's claims comparison (no contradicted done) and the obligation ledger state | slot run, record | green or red; contradicted or not |
| A8 cost and latency | cost USD, turns, duration, tool calls from the result json (all arms); hook latency per event from the record (R, G); the prompt-path timing of the graph brief (G) | result json, record | reported; cost per A4-match run; p95 per hook event |

Primary outcome (pre-registered): A2 and A3 and A4 all pass. Secondary: A1a and A1b rates, A5, A6, A7, A8. The attribution table reports U, R, G and the two differences per metric. Stop rule: n=3 per arm, read, then n=10 per arm unless the n=3 mean cost per run exceeds 2.50 USD. No change to the engine, the knowledge rows, the prompt or the judge after any run; a change opens T1b with its own rows. No lift language below n=10; "signal" at best. The T1 task is preserved: none of the changes above found a problem with it.
